| Content block | M1-B1 | M1 | no | sourceChecked | What cardiac arrest means: During cardiac arrest the heart is no longer pumping blood. | Content block | M1-B2 | M1 | no | sourceChecked | Heart attack and cardiac arrest are different: A heart attack begins with a blocked coronary artery and can progress to cardiac arrest if untreated. Warning signs supported by the source include chest or upper-abdominal tightness or discomfort, possible spread towards the left shoulder or arm, neck or lower jaw, sudden breathlessness, sweating, nausea, vomiting or dizziness. The source directs calling 995 and going to an Emergency Department. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 7; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 8 | fact.recognition.heartAttackVsArrest |
| Content block | M1-B3 | M1 | no | sourceChecked | Minutes matter: Brain cells begin to die within about four to six minutes after cardiac arrest, and irreversible injury may begin at roughly four minutes without restored circulation. The supplied manuals report survival near 90% with immediate reversal, around 40–50% at six minutes and about 10% by nine minutes. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 8; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 16; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 9 | fact.recognition.timeCriticality |
| Content block | M1-B4 | M1 | no | sourceChecked | Shockable rhythms: Ventricular fibrillation and pulseless ventricular tachycardia are shockable rhythms. | Content block | M2-B1 | M2 | no | clinicalReviewRequired | Current learning sequence — seven rings: The course's active, review-gated learning sequence provisionally follows the 2022 provider manual: Prevention; Early Activation and AED Access; Early CPR; Early Defibrillation; Emergency Medical Services (Ambulance); Advanced Cardiac Life Support; Recovery. The two current supplied sources differ on the graphic and ring count, so this card is excluded from scoring until a qualified SME approves the presentation. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 11; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 5; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.chain.sevenRings |
| Content block | M2-B2 | M2 | no | sourceChecked | What the rings mean: Prevention covers reducing risk through a healthy lifestyle and regular checks. Activation means calling 995, following the dispatcher and arranging a nearby visible AED through another person; a lone rescuer stays with the casualty. Early CPR means starting promptly because brain cells can begin dying within four to six minutes. Early Defibrillation means applying a community AED and being ready to shock. EMS and advanced hospital care continue treatment, and Recovery represents rehabilitation and community support. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 11; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 12; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 19; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 31; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.chain.ringDefinitions, fact.drsabc.getAed |

| Content block | M3-B1 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | DRSABC: Move through DRSABC in order: first assess Danger, then test Responsiveness; Shout for help and activate 995; Ask another person for an AED; decide whether Breathing is normal; and begin continuous Chest Compressions when normal breathing is absent or uncertain. A pulse check appears only in the clearly labelled trained-healthcare-provider path. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 17; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 14 | fact.drsabc.mnemonic |
| Content block | M3-B2 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Danger comes first: Work only in a safe environment. | Content block | M3-B6 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Bystander available or alone: When another person is available, ask them to fetch an AED that is within about a 60-second walk. When you are the lone rescuer, stay with the casualty and begin compressions rather than leaving to search for an AED. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 19; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 31 | fact.drsabc.getAed |
| Content block | M3-B7 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Guideline update: the lone rescuer stays: The 2018 manual allowed a lone rescuer to fetch an AED when it was visible and nearby. Current supplied guidance says the lone rescuer must not leave the casualty; another person should retrieve an AED within the stated walking-distance rule. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 19; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 31; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 14; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 24; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.drsabc.getAed |
| Content block | M3-B8 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Breathing branch: Look for chest rise and fall for no more than 10 seconds. Gasping is not normal breathing. If breathing is absent or abnormal, or you are unsure, begin CPR. If breathing is normal, do not compress; keep monitoring while waiting for EMS. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 19; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 15; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 16; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 29; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 11; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 17 | fact.drsabc.breathingCheck, fact.drsabc.monitorIfBreathingNormally, fact.recognition.gaspingAbnormal |
| Content block | M3-B9 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Remote-area exception: If a lone rescuer in a remote area has no way to activate EMS, assess responsiveness and breathing, start compressions immediately when normal breathing is absent, and continue for at least two minutes before leaving to seek help. This is an edge case, not the main flow. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 19 | fact.drsabc.remoteAreaException |
| Content block | M3-B10 | M3 | no | sourceChecked | Healthcare-provider path: Only the HCP-labelled path includes a carotid pulse check together with breathing for no more than 10 seconds. | Content block | M4-B2 | M4 | no | sourceChecked | Landmark and xiphoid avoidance: Locate the lower half of the sternum at the centre of the chest. Expose enough of the chest to place the hands accurately, keeping pressure away from the xiphoid process at the sternum's lower tip. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 20; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 18; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 17 | fact.compression.site |
| Content block | M4-B3 | M4 | no | sourceChecked | Adult hand position and posture: Kneel beside the casualty. | Content block | M4-B4 | M4 | no | sourceChecked | Rate and the 110 practice tempo: The sourced adult compression rate is 100–120 per minute. | Content block | M4-B6 | M4 | no | sourceChecked | Count and control interruptions: Count aloud in cycles of five up to 100 to support rhythm and tracking. Keep compressions continuous. | Content block | M5-B1 | M5 | no | sourceChecked | What the AED does: An AED reads the heart rhythm through its pads, decides whether a shock is needed, charges only for a shockable rhythm and guides the rescuer with voice prompts. | Content block | M5-B2 | M5 | no | sourceChecked | Bring an AED to every arrest: When 995 is activated, direct an available bystander to get an AED. | Content block | M5-B3 | M5 | no | sourceChecked | Power on and follow prompts: Continue compressions while the AED arrives. | Content block | M5-B6 | M5 | no | sourceChecked | Hair at a pad site: If chest hair prevents pad contact, shave the required pad sites promptly using the shaver supplied with the AED kit. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 33; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 26 | fact.aed.chestPrepHairyChest |

| Content block | M6-B1 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Adult AED application — steps 1–10: 1. Keep compressions going as the AED arrives. 2. Open the case. 3. Switch on the AED. 4. Expose the chest. 5. Make the chest and surroundings ready for safe pad contact. 6. Open the pads and follow their pictures. 7. Place the right pad below the right collarbone. 8. Place the left pad below and left of the left nipple. 9. Plug in the connector if it is not pre-connected. 10. Stop contact only when the AED announces analysis, spread your arms and state ‘Stay Clear’. | SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 30; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 35; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 34; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 28; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 33; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 26; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 32; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch, fact.aed.applyDuringCpr, fact.aed.chestPrepJewellery, fact.aed.hazards, fact.aed.padPlacementAdult |
| Content block | M6-B2 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Eleven AED learning states: The academy uses eleven project-authored states to rehearse the sourced flow: Awaiting AED; Power On; Prepare Chest; Apply Right Pad; Apply Left Pad; Connect Pads; Analyse — Clear; Charge — Clear; Clear Check and Shock; No Shock Advised; Resume CPR. These labels organise interaction and do not alter the AED's own prompts. | SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 30; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 35; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 34; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 28; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 37; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch, fact.aed.applyDuringCpr, fact.aed.noShockAdvised, fact.aed.padPlacementAdult, fact.aed.resumeAfterShock, fact.aed.shockDelivery |
| Content block | M6-B3 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Adult pad placement: Match the diagrams on the pads: position one high on the right chest beneath the collarbone and the other lower and farther out on the left chest, below the left nipple. Attach the lead if the AED model requires it. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 34; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 35; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 28 | fact.aed.padPlacementAdult |
| Content block | M6-B4 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Analysis means nobody touches: When the AED announces analysis, stop compressions. Nobody touches the casualty. Spread both arms and clearly state ‘Stay Clear’. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch |
| Content block | M6-B5 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Charge, clear-check and shock: For a shockable rhythm the AED charges itself. Keep everyone clear. When the AED prompts for a shock, say ‘Stay Clear’, visually confirm that nobody is touching the casualty, then press the shock button firmly. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.shockDelivery |
| Content block | M6-B6 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Resume after a shock: Restart chest compressions immediately after the shock and continue until the AED announces its next analysis. Current guidance states that re-analysis repeats every two minutes. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 37; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.resumeAfterShock |
| Content block | M6-B7 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Resume after no-shock advice: If the AED advises no shock, restart continuous compressions immediately. Continue until the emergency team takes over, the AED directs that nobody touch the casualty, or the casualty resumes normal breathing. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 37; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 30 | fact.aed.noShockAdvised |
| Content block | M6-B8 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Keep the AED connected: Leave the AED switched on and connected throughout, including after normal breathing returns and during transport. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 37; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 30 | fact.aed.remainConnected |
| Content block | M6-B9 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Guideline update: two-minute re-analysis: The 2018 flow showed repeat analysis without naming the interval. Current supplied guidance makes the two-minute re-analysis cycle explicit. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 37; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.resumeAfterShock |
| Content block | M6-B10 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Never shock while anyone is touching: Nobody may touch the casualty during analysis, charging or shock. Before choosing the simulated shock action, state the clear command, spread both arms and visually confirm that nobody is touching. The project classifies contact here as a critical error, and the interaction implementation must fail closed unless the clear check is satisfied. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch, fact.aed.shockDelivery |
| Interactive activity | M6-A1 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Pad-placement laboratory: Apply right then left adult pads to the labelled training model, connect if prompted and keep compressions interrupted only when the AED begins analysis. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 34; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 35; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 28; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 30; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch, fact.aed.applyDuringCpr, fact.aed.padPlacementAdult |
| Interactive activity | M6-A2 | M6 | no | sourceChecked | Clear-check rehearsal: State ‘Stay Clear’, scan the clear zone and proceed only when nobody is touching during analysis, charging or shock. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch, fact.aed.shockDelivery |

| Content block | M7-B1 | M7 | no | sourceChecked | Drill 1 — unsafe location: Identify traffic, fire, a wet floor or falling objects. Move the casualty only when the found location is unsafe or unsuitable, then use a safe, flat, open space. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 18; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 15; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.drsabc.danger |

| Content block | M7-B3 | M7 | no | sourceChecked | Drill 3 — metal surface: Move the casualty away from metal contact before AED use because the surface can conduct current towards the rescuer. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 32; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 12; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 26; Docs/PHASE3B_CONTENT_POLICY.md (1.0 (2026-08-07)), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.aed.hazards |

| Content block | M9-B2 | M9 | no | sourceChecked | Why paediatric arrest may differ: The 2022 manual states that paediatric out-of-hospital arrest is usually non-cardiac and most cases begin with a non-shockable rhythm, so ventilation and oxygenation are important. It also reports shockable initial rhythms in about 5–19% of cases, particularly among school-age children and adolescents. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 44 | fact.child.aetiology |
| Content block | M9-B3 | M9 | no | sourceChecked | Early AED awareness: The supplied 2022 manual states that ILCOR supports early AED use for a child in cardiac arrest. It reports higher survival among children with shockable rhythms treated early than among children presenting with other rhythms. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 44 | fact.child.earlyAedAdvocated |
| Content block | M9-B4 | M9 | no | clinicalReviewRequired | Child pad system — SME decision required: The 2022 manual uses an age-based 1–12-year paediatric dose-attenuation rule, while the 2021 summary uses a body-weight threshold below 25 kg. A qualified SME must set one canonical learner-facing rule before this content can be approved. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 44; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 45; 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; Docs/COURSE_SOURCE_DIFFERENCES.md (Draft 2026-08-07), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.child.doseAttenuation, fact.child.padWeightRule |
| Content block | M9-B5 | M9 | no | clinicalReviewRequired | Infant defibrillation — SME decision required: For an infant, the 2021 summary places a manual defibrillator first and child pads next when the preferred device is unavailable. None of the supplied sources gives a clear adult-pad instruction for an infant, so the course withholds one pending SME approval. | 03-SG-BCLSAED-Guidelines-2021.pdf (2021), p. 23; CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 45; Docs/COURSE_SOURCE_DIFFERENCES.md (Draft 2026-08-07), p. n/a (Markdown) | fact.child.infantDefibrillation |
| Content block | M9-B6 | M9 | no | sourceChecked | Adult pads on a child: For a child, the 2022 manual permits an AED with adult pads when no paediatric system is available. Pads must not touch or overlap; with adult pads, use front-and-back placement with one pad on the central sternum and one on the upper back between the shoulder blades. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 45; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 34 | fact.child.adultPadsOnChild |
| Content block | M9-B7 | M9 | no | sourceChecked | Paediatric pad separation: For front-of-chest paediatric pad placement, follow the pad pictures and keep the pads from touching or overlapping, with at least 2 cm between them. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 45 | fact.child.padSeparation |
| Content block | M9-B8 | M9 | no | clinicalReviewRequired | Compression values and methods — SME review: The supplied 2021 summary contains child and infant depth, landmark, method and full-recoil details, but neither provider manual teaches the paediatric technique. | Critical error | scenario-d-community-facility-error-contact-during-analysis | scenario-d-community-facility | yes | sourceChecked | Contact during AED analysis: The learner or another person remains in contact with the casualty while the AED is analysing. Remediation: Repeat the analysis clear sequence: stop compressions, spread the arms, say “Stay Clear”, and confirm nobody is touching. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch |
| Scenario feedback | scenario-d-community-facility-error-contact-during-analysis-feedback | scenario-d-community-facility | yes | sourceChecked | AED analysis requires a complete no-touch clear check. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.analysisNoTouch |
| Critical error | scenario-d-community-facility-error-shock-without-clear-check | scenario-d-community-facility | yes | sourceChecked | Shock attempted without a clear check: The learner presses the shock control without first keeping everybody clear, repeating the clear call and visually confirming no contact. Remediation: Repeat charging and shock delivery with nobody touching and a visible clear check before pressing the control. | CA-Manual-REV-1-2022.pdf (2022), p. 36; SRFAC-CPRHOAED-Manual-2018-2.pdf (2018), p. 29 | fact.aed.shockDelivery |
